### T-6901 · Rollback · rollback provably restores the saved value — not just the panel, the actual setting
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: BF-PRO

Steps: 1. Open /settings, expand the Interface group, and copy the currently SAVED theme/default-page values verbatim. 2. Change the theme selector to a different value and SAVE it — copy the new saved value. 3. Change the theme selector again to a third value but do NOT save. 4. Press the group's rollback control. 5. Read the theme selector's value and copy it verbatim. 6. If the confirmation dialog asked for confirmation, use the cancel path (the accept path is verified in served bytes per the native-dialog rule); if no dialog appeared, record that.

Expected: After rollback the selector shows exactly the step-2 SAVED value — verbatim, not the unsaved third value — proving the rollback restored the saved setting, not merely repainted the panel. Showing the unsaved third value after rollback is a FAIL.

A blank result area after the re-render is a FAIL. (Copy the exact observed wording either way.)

### T-6904 · Rollback · blast radius — rolling back one group leaves other groups' unsaved changes alone
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.

## Section BK · Proactive: settings groups wave-3 — navigation, resets, and the fine print of group chrome (10 cases: T-6959–T-6968)

### T-6978 · Notification history · rows sent before a tunnel rotation keep their original timestamps — no re-dating

### ISS-099 — Duplicate-guard response renders its tail clause twice
Found: 2026-09-27 ~13:49 PDT and ~13:55 PDT, eyes-on UI gates (two independent runs, live tunnel).
Observation (exact observed text, click 2 and click 3): "Draft saved — saved to SmartTrade drafts as drf-dde35e6af59b — pick it up under SmartTrade → MCP drafts. (This is your existing draft from your last identical request — no duplicate was created.) — pick it up under SmartTrade → MCP drafts. (This is your existing draft from your last identical request — no duplicate was created.)" — the tail clause "— pick it up under SmartTrade → MCP drafts. (This is your existing draft from your last identical request — no duplicate was created.)" renders TWICE.
Diagnosis needed: find where the doubling comes from — double-append in _draft_out composition vs two separate UI regions rendering the same clause vs htmx swap artifact. The ISS-098 backend gate counted "no duplicate was created" = 1 in raw HTML and called it a text-extraction artifact; the eyes-on evidence from two independent runs says otherwise — the gate must assert on the exact text the browser renders for the duplicate response, not a substring count on raw HTML alone.
Expected: the duplicate response renders each clause exactly once.
Status: NOT-A-BUG (2026-09-27 ~14:00 PDT) — test artifact, closed on evidence.
ISS-099 FIX WAVE FINAL REPORT (2026-09-27 ~13:57–14:00 PDT, coordinator + 2 verifiers): three convergent probes — (1) served bytes: full 6,957-byte duplicate-response body from live pid 7369, "pick it up under SmartTrade → MCP drafts" = 1, "no duplicate was created" = 1, one drf-id, one ai-draft-confirm element, one #ai-panel root (hypothesis: double-append in _draft_out composition — DEAD); (2) post-swap DOM: jsdom + the exact vendored static/vendor/htmx-2.0.4.min.js, real captured click-1/click-2 bodies, outerHTML-equivalent swap → after click-2: pickup=1, dup-note=1, exactly one #ai-panel root (hypothesis: htmx swap artifact — DEAD); (3) no client script duplicates the text — nothing references .ai-draft-confirm outside app/main.py, nd-toasts only fire for kill/halt flows, form-errors.js only paints into data-err-target regions (hypothesis: second UI region — DEAD). The doubling signature (prefix once + tail twice) is identical across both UI runs — the fingerprint of a systematic text-extraction quirk in the browser automation, not a DOM duplication (which would duplicate whole elements). Residual honesty: the automation's text reader itself is not inspectable, but every inspectable layer — bytes, swap, scripts — shows the clause exactly once, so a real user sees it once. No code changed; server untouched (pid 7369). BACKEND GATE: PASS — 7/7 x 2 independent sequential verifiers on served bytes. Zero provider calls; live ledgers unchanged (honesty 40 rows / jev 7 rows, md5-identical). Evidence artifacts: ~/workspace/iss099-v2-verify/ (iss099_r2.html 6,957 bytes md5 658dad99d6d1d7632ddd6a86e3886ee8, iss099_r1.html, swaptest.js, V2's post1/post2_dup/post_rejected/drafts_before/after).
STANDING LESSON: when counted served bytes AND a swap simulation with the real vendored htmx both show a clause exactly once, an automation-observed text doubling is a text-extraction artifact, not a defect — the gate is the bytes. Precedent: ISS-082. A UI re-run is not informative here because the UI automation itself is the artifact source; the backend gate is the closing evidence.

ISS-099 = NOT-A-BUG (test artifact, closed on counted-bytes + swap-simulation evidence; backend gate 7/7 x 2 PASS). **SECTION AR: CLOSED 2026-09-27 ~14:00 PDT — 17/17 issues resolved through both gates (16 FIXED: ISS-083–098; 1 NOT-A-BUG: ISS-099).** Zero issues open.

### ISS-255 — "1a2b" silently sanitized to "12" and accepted — mixed letters/numbers not refused (T-119)
Found: 2026-09-28 ~21:50 UTC, lane 4 UI run (live tunnel, UI-only), chunk 3.
Exact observed behavior: typing "1a2b" into the money field silently stripped the letters, parsed as 12, and opened the confirm screen — no error, no blocked Start. (Pure garbage like "abc" and "$#@" IS rejected at keystroke, so the field is inconsistent: it refuses some bad input and silently repairs other bad input.)
Why it matters: silent sanitization is the worst of both worlds — the operator typed "1a2b" and the app decided they meant 12 without saying so. On a money field that feeds live orders, a silent reinterpretation is a mis-trade waiting to happen.
Expected: mixed letters-and-numbers input is refused with an error and Start stays blocked — never silently stripped and accepted.
Status: OPEN — fix wave sequenced after the lanes.

The case expects an emergency stop card naming what it stops.

### ISS-291 — Halt indicators contradicted each other on the pre-rotation tunnel (lane 3 chunk 1)
Found: 2026-09-29 ~05:40 UTC, lane 3 UI run (UI-only), observed on the pre-rotation tunnel; post-rotation tunnel showed consistent state.
Exact observed text: header badge read "HALTED · NEW ENTRIES BLOCKED" and the sidebar read "Halted" — while the Risk control panel on the same page read "Trading — entries allowed", "Halt is off — trading is running. Nothing to resume.", with the "Halt trading" button shown. One screen said halted; another said trading.
Why it matters: the halt state is the most dangerous indicator in the software. Two panels disagreeing about whether trading is halted means the operator cannot trust either one — and pressing "Halt trading" to reconcile could arm a halt that isn't needed, or mask a halt that is.
Expected: every surface shows the same engine state from the same source of truth; no page ever shows halted and trading at once. (Post-rotation tunnels were consistent — the fix team should determine whether this was stale-tunnel state or a real race.)
Status: OPEN — fix wave sequenced after the lanes.

### ISS-329 — /history tells the same quantity two ways: "2 BTC" vs "2 contracts (0.0002 BTC)" (T-3749)
Found: 2026-09-29 ~07:00 UTC, lane 19 UI run (live tunnel, UI-only).
Exact observed behavior: on /history, the Receipt log's Qty cells read "2 BTC" for all 3 fills ("logfill-82472172775371612", "logfill-82472172775042722", "logfill-82472172774893106"), while the Trade history region on the same page reads Size "2 contracts (0.0002 BTC)" for the trade built from those fills. 2 BTC (~$168k notional) vs 0.0002 BTC (~$16.79) cannot both be right; against a "USDT balance: $999.80" account with 0.75% risk sizing, "2 contracts (0.0002 BTC)" is the plausible figure. The / page shows the same split — orders table "2 BTC" vs replay fills "2 contracts". Suspected cause: the chunk-1 bare-"2" fix (ISS-261) labeled the quantity with the wrong unit — the quantity is in contracts, not BTC.
Why it matters: a fills table that overstates quantity by 10,000× makes every fill look like a six-figure trade on a $999 account — the operator cannot trust any quantity on the page.
Expected: one quantity, one unit, everywhere — fills, trade history, and orders agree (contracts where the venue trades contracts).
Status: OPEN — fix wave sequenced after the lanes.

### ISS-330 — Header sign-in state disagrees between pages on the no-auth TEST cockpit (lane 19 observation)
Found: 2026-09-29 ~07:00 UTC, lane 19 UI run (live tunnel, UI-only).
Exact observed behavior: the header auth block shows "Signed out" with an "Operator token" textbox + "Sign in" button on /home and /strategies, while the TEST cockpit runs with COCKPIT_NO_AUTH=1 (Saad's 2026-09-23 order: no password gate). Served bytes confirm: /home and /strategies render `data-state="checking"` with no `data-auth-disabled="1"` and no "No sign-in needed" label. Root cause (read-only tree inspection): templates/partials/auth_block.html renders "No sign-in needed" only when the page passes `auth_disabled` into the template context (e.g. /keys via app/main.py); routes in app/simple_ui_routes.py (/home, /trading, /notifications) never pass it, so the JS probe paints "signed-out". The template's own comment says a failed/slow probe "can never mislabel this backend 'Signed out'" — but only for pages that pass the flag.
Why it matters: a tester (or Saad) seeing "Signed out" on the TEST cockpit reasonably concludes they must sign in — the exact confusion the no-auth order was meant to eliminate. The state must be consistent on every page.
Expected: every page on the TEST cockpit renders the same "No sign-in needed" state — pass `auth_disabled` (or a shared context default) on all routes.
Status: OPEN — fix wave sequenced after the lanes.

### ISS-331 — /keys Slot name / Label silently accept 150-character strings; no length limit stated (T-3778/T-3784)

The 150-char value was fully retained (verified via field value read); same in the edit dialog's Label field. Page layout did not break. ### ISS-290 — Failed-message-queue Discard fires on a single click with no confirmation (T-2658/T-2659)

### ISS-289 — Refused-signal "What to do next" contradicts the auto-retry design (T-2498)
Found: 2026-09-29 ~05:30 UTC, lane 13 UI run (live tunnel b128fa0ba7c3bf, UI-only), chunk 2 (T-2451–T-2500).
Exact observed text: "What to do next" reads: "This signal is stale — the strategy's checks since then haven't re-sent it, so no retry is pending. A new signal will only come from a fresh setup. If signals keep failing, note the time (2d ago) and tell the agent team." The case expects: the signal stays eligible and retries automatically, "Check now" retries sooner, and the HTTP code is given — none of that is present.
Why it matters: the card's own line agrees with the disclosure ("This signal is stale — it will not be retried."), so the UI is internally consistent — but the DESIGN contract says auto-retry with "Check now" and the HTTP code. Either the design or the implementation is wrong, and a trader reading "will not be retried" while the design says "retries automatically" cannot know which is true.
Expected: one deliberate decision — the UI and the design agree on whether refused signals auto-retry, whether "Check now" retries sooner, and whether the HTTP code is shown — recorded, not accidental.
Status: FIX STAGED 2026-09-29 — fixer self-gate PASS (defect-mirroring fixture HTTP 500 sent 2d ago: all four retry-truth elements present, 'will not be retried'/'no retry is pending'/'stale' absent; node --check OK). Decision recorded: UI and design agree on auto-retry (plan Section HK T-8336/T-8337). Coordinator claim-check clean. UI gate PENDING post-deploy — checklist in hidden_files/wave4-w3-iss289.md. Files: templates/strategies.html (refusalNext rewritten branch-free around the HTTP code; stale branch + signalIsStale/SIGNAL_STALE_AFTER_S deleted).

## Section BM · Regression: ISS-079–ISS-082 — AI-provider save honesty, health lines, model restore, single render (20 cases: T-6979–T-6998)

Regression coverage for ISS-079 (FIXED — /settings → AI providers saved keys with zero server-side validation and zero save feedback), ISS-080 (FIXED — provider pill still read "OpenRouter Active" right after the UI itself reported a 401), ISS-081 (NOT-A-BUG — model dropdown once showed the wrong selected model after a tunnel rotation; the audit trail proved it was the test task's own earlier flip, and hardening shipped anyway), and ISS-082 (NOT-A-BUG — Test-result block rendered twice in the DOM; proven a text-extraction artifact of the automation on served bytes). All four found 2026-09-27 ~10:48 PDT in the AI-brain batch (T-6569–T-6584); all four closed by the ~10:54 PDT fix wave through both gates. These cases lock the shipped contracts: validate-on-save with the verbatim refusal, the per-panel save-feedback line, persisted per-provider last_check health lines with the "Active" pill provably selection-only, failed-POST UI revert, and the exactly-one-result-block invariant. Written 2026-09-27 ~11:00 PDT.

Boundary for the whole section: the wrong-key save cases use deliberately FAKE keys only — the refusal IS the pass condition, so nothing real is ever at risk. The real saved key (••••3653) must be intact at the end of every case: the indicator must still read "Saved key: ••••••••3653 — pasting a new key and saving replaces it." Never type the real key; never delete a slot; never save a wrong key expecting it to stick.

### T-6979 · ISS-079 regression · saving a deliberately wrong OpenRouter key is refused with the exact verbatim refusal — the fake key is never stored
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: BM-79

Steps: 1. On /settings → AI providers, OpenRouter panel, record the current indicator (••••3653). 2. Paste a deliberately fake key (e.g. sk-or-v1-9999 followed by a random tail — a key that can never be real) into the key field. 3. Press "Save key" and record the response word for word. 4. Record the indicator text immediately after.
Expected: The save is REFUSED — the response reads verbatim: "OpenRouter rejected that key (HTTP 401 — the key was not recognized). The saved key was NOT changed — check the key and try again." No save-success copy appears anywhere. The fake key's last-4 never appears in the indicator or anywhere else. Any acceptance of the fake key, any silence, or any paraphrase of the refusal copy is a FAIL.

### T-6998 · ISS-082 regression · the second run's result replaces the first — exactly one block at all times, never a lingering old result

## Section BN · Proactive: rollback flows wave-3 — races, edges, and offline honesty (10 cases: T-6999–T-7008)

Third-cycle rotation for the rollback flows (wave-1, Section AE T-6303–T-6332; wave-2, Section BF T-6901–T-6912, which swept the core behaviors: saved-value restore, confirmation surviving re-render, no-dirty honesty, blast radius, round trip, per-group dirty indicators, toggles, exact naming, idempotence, sealed-group protection, cross-page agreement, rollback-after-failed-save). This wave sweeps the remaining edges: the save/rollback race, cancel-then-repress half-state, numeric formatting verbatim, reload-mid-dirty, conditionally-revealed fields, rollback while the server is unreachable, sequential two-group confirmation separation, long-list select exact-option rendering, the /notifications-section rollback (zero live side effects), and the equal-value dirty edge. No case saves a value the tester wouldn't want live — every dirty change is either saved deliberately or rolled back to the previously saved value. Written 2026-09-27 ~11:19 PDT.

### T-6999 · Rollback wave-3 · save in flight, then rollback pressed — exactly one winner, honestly named
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: BN-PRO

Steps: 1. In /settings, expand the Interface group and copy the currently SAVED theme value. 2. Change the theme selector to a different value and press SAVE — immediately (before the save result appears) press the group's rollback control. 3. Wait for all activity to settle, then read the theme selector's value, the save result area, and the rollback result area verbatim.

Expected: Exactly one of the two operations is reported as the winner — either the save landed (selector shows the new value with a "Saved" confirmation and no rollback claim) or the rollback won (selector shows the old saved value with the rollback confirmation and no save claim). Both operations claiming success — "Saved" AND "Rolled back group …" — is a FAIL. A silently dropped operation with no word anywhere is also a FAIL.

---

### T-7000 · Rollback wave-3 · cancel the confirm, then press rollback again — no half-state left behind
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: BN-PRO

Steps: 1. In /settings, expand the Risk caps group, change one cap value (unsaved), and press the group's rollback control. 2. If a native confirm appears, let the automation layer take the cancel path (auto-dismissed); if no dialog appears, record that. 3. Read the cap field's value verbatim — the unsaved change must still be there. 4. Press the rollback control a second time. 5. Read the field and the result area verbatim.

Expected: After step 2 the unsaved value is intact (cancel = true no-op, per the ISS-077 cancel-branch rule). After step 4 the field shows the SAVED value and the "Rolled back group …" confirmation appears — the second press behaves exactly like a first press, proving the canceled first press left no half-state. A second press that reports success without changing anything, or that throws an error, is a FAIL.
